PART IV. NOTIFICATIONS, During the Quarter ended September 30, 1905.
NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA &#39;SEA. o. . SHANGHAI DSTRICT. SOUTHEN ENTRANCE TO THE YANGTZE. NoTiEw is hereby given that, owing to the washing away of the bank, the Quarantine Becn on Chung-pao-sha Island has been shifted 4.5 eable S . 54&#39; E. from its former position. SANGAkI,6th July igoS-. No. 40. YANGTZE RVER-KI U KIANG DISTRICT. TUNGLIU BEACON. NoTicE is hereby given that, owing to the washing away of the river bank, the Tungliti Baon has been shifted 225 feet east from its former position. SHANGHAI, I I th July 1905.
1905.J NOTIFICATIONS. 237 No. 408. SWATOW DISTRICT. ROCK TO THE NORTH OF LAMOCK ISLANDS. The following information has been received from the Commander of H.B.M.&#39;s surveying- ship Waterwitch:&mdash; &quot;A rock, with 20 feet over it at L.W.O.S. and depths of 8 to 17 fathoms close round, is situated in the following position: &quot; From the rock&mdash; &quot; Lamock Island High Light bears S. 70 W. &quot; Centre of Dome Island bears S. 65&deg; W., distant 11.4 miles. &quot; North Point Namoa Island bears N. 730 W. &quot; The rock is marked by tide rips during the strength of the tide. The lead gives no warning of approach. Bearings are magnetic&mdash; &quot;Latitude 230 26&#39; N. &quot;Longitude 1170 19&#39;E.&quot; Shanghai, 22nd July 1905. No. 409. CHEFOO DISTRICT. TOWER HILL LIGHT EXHIBITED. Notice is hereby given that the Tower Hill Light, Chefoo Harbour, was exhibited for the first time at sunset on the 24th July. The illuminating apparatus is dioptric, occulting, of the third order, showing a lixed white and red light varied by a single eclipse every 10 seconds, thus:&mdash; Light &bdquo; 8 seconds. Eclipse 2 &bdquo; The lantern, which is painted white, surmounts the Native tower on the summit of Tower Hill; and the light, which is elevated 175 feet above the level of the sea, should be visible in clear weather at a distance of 19^ miles, except where it is obscured by land. The light shows red from S. 340 W. to S. 4if&deg; W.; white from S. 4if&deg; W. to S. 590 W.; red from S. 590 W. to N. 8o&deg; W., except where it is obscured by the islands of Kungtungtao; ivhite from N. 8o&deg; W. to N. 720 W.; and red from N. 720 W. to where it is cut off by the land. It also shows red, within the harbour only, from S. 25&deg; E. to S. 340 W. Bearings are magnetic and from seaward. Approximate position:&mdash; Latitude 370 33&#39; N. Longitude 121&deg; 25&#39;E. Shanghai, 19th August 1905.
